# MODLOG

Running log of what changed and what was tested. Newest first. "Tested" means I ran it and looked at a screenshot or the importer's output. I cannot see the game live, so every visual check is a screenshot of the start line (or the spot named). Where I did not check something, it says "not tested".

Written 2026-10-17, after the fact, from the session's commits and my notes. Entries before 2026-10-16 are summarised from memory of the earlier part of this session, so their detail is thinner.

Importer changes (`crates/fh1setup/...`) were made in the sibling worktree `D:\Projects\slop\fh1_rewrite_fm3` on branch `fm2-lightmaps`. Engine changes (`crates/fh1-engine`, `crates/fh1-remaster`) are in this folder on branch `ui-elements`.

Not committed right now (uncommitted in the fm3 worktree): the Suzuka road gain, the wear-invert rule and the matching survey/debug knobs. See the first three entries.

## 2026-10-17 Gamma-space lightmap product tried on all 12 maps (`FH1_RM_FM2_GAMMA_LM=1`, not adopted, UNCOMMITTED knob in `material.rs`)

**Changed:** nothing new; the engine knob added earlier was run on every map.
**Why:** Sunset Infield's road is 0.43x Xenia; its texture (mean 92) times the game's lightmap blend in display space (about 1.1) gives about 101, close to Xenia's 104, while the engine's linear product gives 74.
**Tested how:** screenshots of all 12 maps with the knob on, road-band ratio against Xenia (ours / Xenia, linear).
**Result:** closer on 3 of 12, farther on the rest: Sunset Infield 0.43 to 0.66, New York 0.41 to 0.93 (road colour 92,61,49 against Xenia 95,63,51), Tsukuba 1.09 to 0.99; but Silverstone 1.56 to 2.37, Road Atlanta 1.38 to 0.52, Laguna Seca 1.36 to 2.27, Mugello 0.56 to 0.37, Test Track 0.66 to 0.54, Suzuka 2.06 to 2.60, Nurburgring 1.58 to 1.84.
**Still broken / not tested:** no single global colour-space rule fits all maps. The maps it helps (New York, Sunset Infield, Tsukuba) are the ones with low light colours (about 1.2 or less); the ones it hurts mostly have larger light colours (Laguna Seca 1.47, Silverstone 1.37), so the lightmap light/dark colours may be applied differently from the engine's lerp. Not tested.
**Next:** check how the game applies `DarkColor` / `LightColor` (the shader's `c184` / `c185` lerp) against the engine's `lm * (light - dark) + dark`.

## 2026-10-17 Sunset Infield start-line chequer (cut-out decal) (UNCOMMITTED)

**Changed:** `fm2_scenery.rs` (wear-blend pass): a wear-blend draw whose base colour is a cut-out texture (in `keyed_ids`) and whose shader alpha is not constant is drawn as a plain cut-out card (`h_diff_opac_1_lm`), not as a wear blend. `FM2_NO_DECAL_CUTOUT=1` turns it off.
**Why:** Sunset Infield's start line (model 36, a 2-triangle decal with texture 96: grey squares opaque, the others transparent) came out as a solid pale band; Xenia shows a black-and-white chequer.
**Tested how:** `FM2_UNDER` at the start; screenshot of the spawn view; all 12 maps re-imported and shot; road-band ratios against Xenia and whole-frame differences compared with a noise baseline (two runs with no intended change differ by 0.3 to 4.8 per pixel from road grain and animation).
**Result:** chequer drawn across the road. Road-band ratios unchanged on all maps (Sunset Infield 0.44 to 0.43, Laguna Seca 1.29 to 1.36, Suzuka 2.00 to 2.06, others within 0.05). New York's frame difference was above its baseline (7.4 against 3.7), but side by side the two shots look the same.
**Still broken / not tested:** Sunset Infield's road is still dark brown (0.43x Xenia): its base texture (252) is a mid-dark gravel and I have not looked at its lightmap or colours. The tyre-streak darkening on that card is dropped (the decal has none in Xenia).

## 2026-10-17 Test Track road fixed (placeholder textures) and Laguna Seca road lightened (UNCOMMITTED)

**Changed:** `fm2_scenery.rs`: (1) runtime textures (PVS flags 1) of at most 32x32 that are not BC4 masks are no longer used as colour layers (`placeholder_ids`, 34 across the maps; `FM2_KEEP_PLACEHOLDER=1` restores); (2) the wear-invert rule now applies to any draw with a real colour texture and a bright (> 140) wear mask, not only tiled ones; (3) debug `FM2_UNDER=x,z,y` lists the draws under a point with their material.
**Why:** Test Track's road draw (model 122) listed texture 24, a 1x1 black placeholder, as its base colour; the wear step could not make a darkened copy of it, so the second layer fell back to white and the road came out pale. Laguna Seca's road (model 168) is grey asphalt with a bright wear mask, so the old order darkened almost the whole road.
**Tested how:** `FM2_UNDER` at start-line spots, texture views, then all 12 maps re-imported and a median of two bare-road bands compared with Xenia (ours / Xenia, linear).
**Result:** Test Track 2.58 to 0.67; Laguna Seca 0.27 to 1.29; the other ten maps unchanged. Both numbers are from road bands only.
**Still broken / not tested:** Silverstone has a large flat black patch left of the white line (Xenia: grey tarmac). Turning wear off, and turning the baked-road texture off, left it unchanged, so it is neither; the draws probed there (models 37 and 97) are a baked-road blend and a grass draw, so the patch is probably a lightmap or an unprobed draw. Two attempts, so recorded here, not chased further. Laguna Seca at 1.29 is now a little bright.

## 2026-10-17 Road colour from the real pixel shader (opt-in `FM2_SHADER_ROAD=1`, UNCOMMITTED)

**Changed:** new `crates/fh1-shaders/src/ps_cpu.rs` (CPU interpreter for FM2 pixel shaders, ALU semantics from `wgsl.rs`, plus `fog_register`); `fm2_uv.rs` hands each draw's pixel shader (`PsRun`: microcode, literals, item to sampler and sampler to interpolator maps) to the importer; `fm2_scenery.rs` `shader_albedo()` runs it on up to 24 vertices with neutral lighting (lightmap blend = 1, sun = 0, fog off) and, only when `FM2_SHADER_ROAD=1`, bakes that mean colour for baked roads instead of the `c1` heuristics. Probe: `crates/fh1setup/examples/fm2_ps_run.rs`. Plan added to `docs/DESIGN.md`.
**Why:** road brightness against the Xenia shots differed by track in both directions (Test Track and Nurburgring about 2.5x too bright; Laguna Seca, Sunset Infield, Mugello about 0.4x).
**Tested how:** sweeps of the interpreter's inputs (output is exactly proportional to the lightmap blend, independent of ambient; the sun term adds on top), then re-import of all 12 maps and a median of two bare-road bands (left and right of the car) compared with Xenia (ours / Xenia, linear):
- before to after: Tsukuba 1.09 to 2.17 (worse), Silverstone 1.55 to 2.45 (worse), Mugello 0.56 to 2.16 (worse), Suzuka 2.00 to 1.06 (better), Maple Valley 4.25 to 7.11 (worse); Test Track 2.58, Nurburgring 1.58, Sunset Infield 0.44, Sebring 0.94, Road Atlanta 1.38, New York 0.41, Laguna Seca 0.27 unchanged.
**Result:** not adopted as default. Test Track's and Nurburgring's road colour does not come from the baked-road branch at all (unchanged after the switch); Test Track's blends use a black 1x1 placeholder (texture 24) as their first colour, so the pale road comes from elsewhere. Laguna Seca's 921 `c0` = 0 road draws evaluate to black in the interpreter (probably overlay passes), so they fall back to the old path.
**Still broken / not tested:** which draws paint the Test Track and Nurburgring roads under the car; Xenia's roads show much more detail (tyre marks, patches) than our flat roads, so some of the "brightness" gap may be contrast and detail.
**Next:** find the draws under the car on Test Track by hiding draws one at a time; look at contrast and detail.

## 2026-10-17 Test Track pale road: cause found, not fixed

**Changed:** nothing in code. Test textures were painted solid colours in the installed data and restored from a backup (`data/work/tt_tex_backup`).
**Why:** Xenia's Test Track road is mid-dark grey asphalt; ours is pale beige.
**Tested how:** painted every texture a solid colour and shot the start line; painted a handful of candidates (43, 45, 46, 51, 53) separately; read the pixel shader of model 16 (`fm2_show`).
**Result:** the road is not drawn from a colour texture. Painting texture 53 green turned the whole road green: 53 is the draw's baked lightmap tile, so the road is "baked road colour x a bright lightmap". The road draws (models 16, 43, 516, 546...) list only BC4 one-channel textures and carry pixel-shader constants `c1` = 1.2, `c2` = 0.1, `c3` = 0.1, `c4` = 0.3, `c5` = 2.0 (other road draws: `c1` = -0.05, `c3` = 0.6, `c5` = 2.5). The shader fetches three of those textures with `.xyz` and blends them with the constants. The importer treats `c1` as the road's colour, so 1.2 gives near-white.
**Still broken / not tested:** nothing fixed. A correct fix means reading how the three layers and `c0..c5` combine in that shader (the Suzuka `c3` gain was a partial version of the same idea). Texture 46 turned out to be the yellow line, not the road.
**Next:** decode the 3-layer road shader (or add a per-map road colour as a stopgap).

### Follow-up: first decode attempt (nothing changed in code)

Read the full pixel shader of model 16, group 2 (the road family with `c1` = 1.2). It fetches `tf4`, `tf5`, `tf6` (colour, `.xyz`), `tf3` (a mask) and `tf2`, then builds three palette terms from one sample `v = c0 - tf6.rgb`: `v*c5`, `v*c4 + c3`, `v*c2`, and `v*c1 - v*c2`; the `tf5` luma (weights 0.114 / 0.299 / 0.587) picks between them, and the result is multiplied by the sun and ambient terms. So `c1..c5` are palette gains and offsets, NOT a road colour: the importer's `c1`-as-colour reading is wrong for this family. Not resolved: `c0` is also multiplied into the sun term (line 64) and is 0 on some draws and 1 on others, so what each constant means per draw depends on the group; the swizzles in the dump (`r11.zxyy` and similar) make hand-reading error-prone. Two ways forward: write a small interpreter that runs the shader's microcode on the sampled textures (data-driven, large), or pick a per-map road colour from the Xenia shot (quick, guesswork).

## 2026-10-17 Wear-invert narrowed to tiled colour textures (UNCOMMITTED)

**Changed:** `fm2_scenery.rs`: the swap now applies only when the wear mask is bright (mean > 140), the draw has a real colour texture (not a baked road) and that colour is tiled over the draw (uv extent >= 4). Debug print of bright-wear draws removed.
**Why:** the first version (entry below) also hit Laguna Seca and Test Track roads (one-pass colours, extent about 1.0) and made them pale; the new Xenia shots show Laguna's road is mid-grey asphalt and Test Track's is mid-dark grey.
**Tested how:** re-imported all maps; screenshots of Suzuka, Laguna Seca, Test Track (opened). Tsukuba and Sebring taken, not opened.
**Result:** Suzuka's tan verge strip kept; Laguna Seca's road dark again like before the rule (still browner and darker than Xenia's); Test Track's road is still pale beige, so that is a separate bug that predates the rule.
**Still broken / not tested:** Test Track road colour (Xenia: mid-dark grey asphalt); the dark band beside Suzuka's edge line is still there.

## 2026-10-17 Wear-blend polarity inverted for bright masks (first version; superseded by the entry above)

**Changed:** `crates/fh1setup/src/fm2_scenery.rs`: a "wear" mask whose mean brightness is above 140 swaps the blend's two layers (`dark_invert`). `FM2_NO_WEAR_INVERT=1` turns it off.
**Why:** Suzuka's sandy verge looked wrong. Its wear mask is a 512x1024 grass-blade detail image (mean 193), not a tyre-streak mask (Road Atlanta's is 38), so darkening by it darkened almost the whole strip.
**Tested how:** Counted wear masks per map (before the field-number fix my first count showed everything as low). Bright masks: Suzuka 231, Test Track 198, Laguna Seca 175, New York 23, Maple Valley 13, Nurburgring 10, Tsukuba 4. Re-imported all maps, screenshots of Suzuka, Laguna Seca, Test Track, New York.
**Result:** Suzuka's verge now tan with a grass edge, like the Xenia shot, but a dark band now runs along the road edge next to the white line. Laguna Seca's road went from dark brown to pale flat beige-grey (Xenia's is mid-grey): not clearly better. Test Track's road also went pale. New York looked unchanged.
**Still broken / not tested:** Maple Valley, Nurburgring and Tsukuba shots from this run not opened. The rule also hits road draws on Laguna Seca and Test Track, probably wrongly (swap exposes the light base texture instead of the baked road). Proposed fix: apply it only where the base is a real colour texture, not a baked road. The user is testing in game before I decide.
**Next:** restrict the rule (or turn it off), then look at the dark band on Suzuka.

## 2026-10-17 Keep the files' triangle winding (commit 442209b)

**Changed:** `fm2_scenery.rs`: the importer no longer flips a draw's winding by an up-facing majority; `FM2_FLIP=1` restores it. Added probe `crates/fh1-formats/examples/fm2_state.rs`.
**Why:** Laguna Seca's gantry sign text was mirrored and a sign board was missing. The flip turned gantries (undersides face down) inside out.
**Tested how:** Census of draws with a clear up/down majority on Tsukuba, Road Atlanta, Sebring, Suzuka (mostly up-facing: e.g. Sebring 1946 up vs 71 down; Nurburgring census printed blank, not tested). Re-imported all maps, start-line shots of 12.
**Result:** Laguna Seca sign reads correctly, missing board appears. Tsukuba grandstand roof, Suzuka gantry and signs, Silverstone fence, Sunset Infield grandstands appear. No roads or terrain went missing in the opened shots.
**Still broken / not tested:** winding only checked at start lines; a one-off flipped draw elsewhere is possible.

## 2026-10-17 Real-time sun shadows on FM2 + sky-dome fix (commit 5d0e3a2 shader, local only; `fm2_sky.rs` fix UNCOMMITTED)

**Changed:** `crates/fh1-remaster/src/material.rs`: FM2's lightmap path samples the sun's shadow map and darkens only where the lightmap is bright (`FH1_RM_FM2_DYN_SHADOW`, default 0.6). `crates/fh1-engine/src/fm2_sky.rs` (untracked file): sky dome marked `NotShadowCaster` and `NotShadowReceiver`. Sheen skips shadowed ground.
**Why:** FM2 scenery is drawn unlit from baked lightmaps, so cars and props cast no shadow.
**Tested how:** Many debug builds painting the shadow value, cascade index, shadow-volume test and the raw stored depth.
**Result:** the shadow value was 0 everywhere because the sky dome I added earlier encloses the scene and sat at the sun's near plane. With it excluded, the car's shadow shows on Tsukuba, Mugello and Test Track; a gantry shadow band shows on Road Atlanta.
**Failed on the way (about six attempts):** reading light 0 (the sun is not light 0, only the second has shadows on); filter method and caching (not the cause); my own cascade maths (fine).
**Still broken / not tested:** only a few maps looked at. `fm2_sky.rs` is untracked, so a clean checkout will not have the fix.

## 2026-10-19: road brightness pipeline diagnosis (no code change)
Read cal_5..cal_9 and dbg_0..4 (Sunset Infield): engine stages agree with their own formulas (albedo 0.34, term 0.37, product 0.126); Xenia needs ~0.2. Sun-height x LightColor does not correlate with per-track ratios. Written up in STATUS.md. Not tested: shader-derived sun term.

## 2026-10-19: FM2 skips FH1's colour grade
**Changed:** `crates/fh1-engine/src/main.rs`: FM2 tracks set `FH1_RM_TONEMAP=none`, `FH1_RM_LUT=0`, `FH1_RM_VIGNETTE=0` unless set; `FH1_RM_FM2_GRADE=1` restores the old look (LUT 0.3, filmic curve).
**Why:** the CPU pixel-shader interpreter on Sunset's road gives the Xenia pixel (113,110,102 vs 111,110,98); the engine's own stages matched their formulas; FH1's curve darkened the road to 73.
**Tested how:** one shot per track, median of road bands vs the Xenia shots, linear-light ours/Xenia. Before -> after: Test Track 0.62->1.0, Sunset 0.36->0.74, New York 0.41->1.11, Mugello 0.56->0.98, Laguna 1.36->0.82 (better); Tsukuba 1.05->1.8, Nurburgring 2.26->3.5, Maple Valley (shot differs) 6.8, Suzuka 1.89, Road Atlanta 1.6 (worse: too bright); Silverstone 0.32, Sebring 0.55 (too dark). Sum of |ln ratio| over 12 tracks 9.9 -> 7.2. Not tested: a real race, other FH1 tracks (change is FM2-only).
**Open:** bright roads need their own fix (Nurburgring, Maple Valley, Tsukuba, Suzuka, Road Atlanta; Xenia also adds a warm haze/bloom on Atlanta), dark ones (Silverstone black patch, Sebring).

## 2026-10-19: copied CPU shader interpreter into this checkout; gamma probe switch
**Changed:** added `crates/fh1-shaders/src/ps_cpu.rs` and `crates/fh1setup/examples/fm2_ps_run.rs` (copied from the `fm2-lightmaps` worktree, where they were uncommitted), `pub mod ps_cpu;` in `crates/fh1-shaders/src/lib.rs`, and the probe-only `PS_GAMMA` env switch in `fm2_ps_run.rs`. No importer or engine change.
**Tested how:** `cargo build -p fh1setup --example fm2_ps_run` ok; Nurburgring model 118 group 0 draw 0 baseline 0.211 reproduced; with PS_GAMMA=3,4,5 gives 0.066 (Xenia 0.058). Sunset model 77 group 2 draw 3 goes black with PS_GAMMA. Details in STATUS.md. Not tested: engine, other tracks.

## 2026-10-19: gamma-per-draw investigation (probes only, no importer/engine change)
**Changed:** new probe `crates/fh1setup/examples/fm2_bind_dump.rs`; `fm2_ps_run.rs` prints draw state records.
**Found:** no gamma bit in draw state, `0x2d` binding flags, PVS flags or `.bix` words. Gamma-decoding only the BC4 grey samplers brings Nurburgring (64 vs Xenia ~70) and Tsukuba (76, was too bright) close and leaves Sunset fine; gamma on Sunset's BC1 colour breaks it. Hypothesis: BC4 = sRGB. Details in STATUS.md.
**Tested how:** `/tmp/lmgrid.sh` lightmap-grid sweeps with `PS_GAMMA` per sampler. Not tested: engine render, other tracks, Test Track.

## 2026-10-19: FM2_BC4_SRGB switch (off by default), Nurburgring road
**Changed:** `crates/fh1setup/src/fm2_scenery.rs`: with `FM2_BC4_SRGB=1`, draws with one BC1-3 colour texture plus two small square grey BC4 textures get a baked colour texture `gain * lerp(sRGB-decoded grey means, by colour luma)` (`FM2_ROAD_GAIN` = gain, `FM2_BC4_SRGB_W=1` also decodes the luma). Default behaviour unchanged.
**Why:** the shader reading (STATUS.md) says the road albedo is lerp of the two BC4 greys by the BC1 texture's luma; sRGB-decoding the greys matched Xenia in the CPU interpreter.
**Tested how:** re-imported Nurburgring with the switch on, rebuilt remaster tables, release-engine screenshot of the grid view, road-band medians: off 0.192 linear, on 0.042 (Xenia about 0.0575). Then re-imported with the switch off (baseline reproduced 0.192). Not tested: other tracks, a race, grey detail tiling.
**Revert:** delete the `FM2_BC4_SRGB` block, or just leave the env var unset.

## 2026-10-19: FM2 post verified against the game's code, blend computed from the frame (`FH1_FM2_POST=1`, off by default)
**Changed:** `crates/fh1-remaster/src/post.rs`: `Fm2Post` gets `lum_min` / `lum_inv_range` from `TrackLumLimits`; the grade shader computes the dark / light blend `t` from an 8 x 6 grid of the frame (override `FH1_FM2_POST_T`). ## 2026-10-19: traced the luminance texture of the game's final combine (no code change)
`DownSample16X` / `DownSample16XAndAccumulate` average the scene colour (same units as the final combine's input) with temporal smoothing, so the blend input is the frame's average luminance; our frames average well below every track's `TrackLumLimits`. Post curve cannot explain the roads' brightness gap (STATUS.md). Not tested: HDR sky.

## 2026-10-19: inherited constants checked (probe only)
The one constant each road shader reads but its draw does not set is the specular exponent register (c2 / c8 / c4 / c6); it is used only in the specular term (killed by c0 = 0 on Mugello). Inherited values cannot be recovered statically and do not change the lit-texel predictions. New probe `fm2_ps_consts.rs`. Not tested: frame-order replay.

## 2026-10-19: material block constants read (probe/script only)
The missing specular exponents are in each model's material block (Nurburgring 9.68, Mugello 9.68, Suzuka 8.47, Tsukuba 12.31, Test Track 7.78). Interpreter with the real exponents and sun geometry: the sun term adds at most +0.004 to the lit road, so it is not the cause of the remaining gaps. No code change; scratch scripts `data/work/mat_consts.py`, `data/work/sun_real.py`.

## 2026-10-19: texture gamma in the game's code (no code change)
The `.bix` format word has no sign bits; the `0x2d` record applies a per-binding sampler-state block from a model-local table, and the sampler-state block carries the sign/gamma bits. So gamma is a per-binding setting in the model's state table, not a format property (STATUS.md). Not done: decoding the state tables (hashed state names).

## 2026-10-19: per-model state tables decoded (no code change)
The `0x2d` index selects identical, empty state entries (Nurburgring, Tsukuba), so it does not carry gamma; hashes unidentified. Earlier claim corrected in STATUS.md. Not done: the texture header builder and set-texture code.

## 2026-10-19: texture header builder read (no code change)
`0x823c5e00` / `0x8236b6d0` take the sign bits only from the format word (zero in the files); no format-dependent gamma. The BC4 proxy is not explained by the texture code; a Xenia GPU trace would decide it. See STATUS.md.

## 2026-10-26: FM2 lightmap tile crop reads a negative scale as a clamp flag (absolute crop)

**Changed:** `fm2_scenery.rs` `convert_lightmaps`: tile region = offset + |scale| * t (the game shader's `|c170|`); `FM2_LM_MIRROR=1` restores the old mirrored crop. Diagnostics: `FM2_LM_SEAMS=1` (lightmap jump across tile seams), `FM2_LM_FLIPU` / `FM2_LM_FLIPV`.
**Why:** shader code and the Sebring page layout; seam jumps drop on 5 of 6 tracks.
**Result:** band sum 16.02 -> 16.20 (Sebring strip now too bright, Suzuka worse, Test Track left 0.96 -> 0.77); seam metric better. All FM2 tracks re-imported, remaster tables rebuilt.

**Revert:** `FM2_LM_MIRROR=1` and re-import.

## 2026-10-26 (3): FM2 lightmap uv from the vertex shader

**Changed:** `fm2_uv.rs` resolves the lightmap tile's uv through the draw's vertex shader (the interpolator the pixel shader clamps for the tile transform); `fm2_scenery.rs` uses it for the lightmap (`FM2_LM_UV_OLD=1` = old guess). Diagnostics `FM2_LM_UV_VS`, `FM2_LM_UV_SIGNED` (seam test only).
**Result:** flat-ground lightmap seams Test Track 0.462 -> 0.101, Mugello 0.187 -> 0.091; baked shadows (Suzuka, Maple Valley) now where Xenia has them. Band sum 16.02 -> 17.86 (brightness rules were tuned against the old lightmaps).
**Test it:** `FM2_LM_SEAMS=1 FM2_TRACKS=mugello ./target/debug/fh1setup.exe import-fm2 data/work_fm2/disc --only maps` prints `flat ground only: ... mean jump 0.0911`; `./target/release/fh1-engine.exe --track fm2/suzuka_00` shows the dark shadow left of the car.
**Revert:** `FM2_LM_UV_OLD=1` and re-import.

## 2026-10-26 (35): FM2's UI scene files (`.xgf`) read; HUD layout, textures, fonts and meshes imported from the disc
**Changed:** `fh1-formats/src/xgf.rs` (new: the compiled Anark scene format of `Media/UI.zip/Scenes`: node records `[first_child, next_sibling, tag, first_attr]`, attribute triples, string table, then the same mesh blob as FH1's `.fbf`), examples `xgf_dump`, `xgf_meshes`, `xds_info`, test `tests/xgf_hud.rs`; `fh1setup/src/fm2_ui.rs` + `import-fm2 --only ui` (writes `imported/fm2/ui/hud.json`, 42 HUD textures as PNG, the vector fonts, string labels, the tach meshes and the `TachHatches` script settings); `fh1-shaders/examples/fm2_ps_consts.rs` accepts `sky`.
**How the layout is found (checked, not assumed):** the file's own nesting does not say which component an item belongs to; the importer takes a container's content as the node after it (or its children) and gives an item the innermost container whose content holds the item's whole unit. Positions add up (scale is not inherited for positions). Checked against the Xenia shots: LAPS panel, RACE / Lap rows, gear and speed digits and the tach plate land on the measured pixels (gear digit x 534 vs 535 measured).
**Tested:** `cargo test -p fh1-formats --test xgf_hud` (node and attribute counts equal the headers on three HUD scenes; all 52 meshes decode, indices in range). Not tested: other scenes (menus) beyond parsing the HUD ones.
**Test it:** `./target/debug/fh1setup.exe import-fm2 data/work_fm2/disc --only ui` prints `42 textures, hud.json written`.
**Revert:** delete the files; the engine falls back to FH1's HUD when `ui/hud.json` is missing.

## 2026-10-26 (36): FM2 circuits draw FM2's own in-race HUD (LAPS, RACE / Lap timer, tach, boost gauge)
**Changed:** `ui/fm2_hud.rs` (new), `ui/fm2_dial.rs` (new), `ui/launch.rs` (`raster_in`: text pictures framed on the font's ascent / descent), `ui.rs` / `ui/hud.rs` / `ui/statushud.rs` / `race/hud.rs` (FH1's HUD, status strip and event prompt stay off while `Fm2HudOn`). `FH1_FM2_HUD=0` keeps FH1's HUD; `FH1_FM2_HUD_ALPHA=0` leaves the dark panels' alpha as stored.
**From the game's files:** layout, textures, fonts, labels (`RACE`, `Lap`, `LAPS`, `MPH` from the string tables), the tach script's rules (read from `I_HUD_TachHatches.lua` bytecode with `data/work/luadis.py`: bucket, number divisor, odd-top rule, tick sets, 250 degree sweep, red zone = arc seen through a sliding texture), needle colour (208, 3, 13) and the red arc's 50 % opacity (the material *instance* overrides the library's).
**Not from the files (inferred, say so):** the needle sweep is linear (the animation's bezier tangents are ignored); the sign conventions of the scene's rotations were fixed by the Xenia shots (0 at the bottom, clockwise); dark panels use alpha x 1.146 converted to linear coverage because the Xenia panels come out 0.38 x the picture behind them on four tracks where the textures' alpha (54 %) would give 0.46 (cause unknown); the boost gauge's value (vacuum -0.75 atm at closed throttle plus the sim's boost) and range (-1 .. `StockBoost-bar` x 0.987) are guesses; rows after RACE and Lap (Best, Last) are not shown.
**Not built:** the minimap (the game draws it with a native renderer, not in the scene), the white fuel arc, the place panel, assist icons beyond ABS / TCS / STM (shown only when the setting is on), countdown lights, pause / results screens, split screen.
**Tested:** Test Track, Tsukuba, Silverstone shots against `screenshots/xenia2/x2, x1, x4`: gauge numbers, ticks, red zone, needle, boost numbers, panels line up (`data/work/cmp_hud.py <ours> <xenia> <prefix>` writes side-by-side crops). The HUD on the other nine tracks: see (37). Race clock and lap rows run from `RaceState` (`FH1_RACE=0`): not tested with a driven race.
**Test it:** `FH1_DIAG=0 FH1_OWNERSHIP=0 FH1_SHOT=screenshots/t.png FH1_SHOT_AT=28 ./target/release/fh1-engine.exe --track fm2/testtrackinfield_00`, then compare the tach with `screenshots/xenia2/x2.png` (log lines `fm2 hud: tach dial 0.0 .. 9500.0 ...`, `boost dial -1.0 .. 0.9`).
**Revert:** the commit, or `FH1_FM2_HUD=0`.

## 2026-10-26 (37): FM2 sky passes are blended in file order
**Changed:** `fm2_sky.rs`: the sky model's draws are drawn alpha-blended, in order (`depth_bias` = index), instead of opaque; `FH1_FM2_SKY_LAYERS=0` = old.
**Why:** Tsukuba's five dome draws share one bounding box and their textures carry partial alpha (clouds, haze): they are passes over the same sphere, not sectors.
**Tested:** Tsukuba and Silverstone shots: the sky is lighter. The clouds are still much weaker than Xenia's; the sky shader (`ps` 1668) takes `c_skyMultiplier` (`skyGain` 0.4) and `c_globalSpecularLevel`; the output alpha as decoded is negative, so the blend mode is not alpha: the real blend is not worked out. Other tracks: not checked one by one.

**Changed:** `ui/fm2_minimap.rs` (new), `ui/fm2_hud.rs` (spawns it, redraws each frame). ## 2026-10-26 (40): sky layer strength tried (`FH1_FM2_SKY_PASSES`), not adopted
**Changed:** `fm2_sky.rs`: `FH1_FM2_SKY_PASSES=n` (default 1) draws each sky layer n times (alpha about n x; the textures' alpha tops out at 0.44).
**Tested:** six tracks, sky band median colour against Xenia (n = 1 / 3): Tsukuba X (120,142,162) vs (141,174,210) / (130,161,196); Sunset X (221,244,254) vs (144,178,216) / (133,162,196); Mugello X (220,229,230) vs (142,173,205) / (137,165,196); Silverstone X (211,213,190) vs (186,207,226) / (192,201,198). More passes move Tsukuba and Silverstone towards Xenia and Sunset / Mugello away from it, so no single strength fits. The sky shader (`ps` 1668) outputs alpha `-a * c_globalSpecularLevel.w` with `c255.x = 0`, so the real blend state (not decoded) decides; Xenia's sun-side skies are near white, ours stay blue. Left at 1.

## 2026-10-26 (42): FM2 post-process moved to gamma-coded values; sky findings (UNCOMMITTED, flag still off by default)
**Changed:** `fh1-remaster/src/post.rs`: the `FH1_FM2_POST=1` branch (desaturate, tint, luminance tone curve, dark/light blend) now works on sRGB-coded values (and takes the frame average for `t` in coded values) instead of linear ones. `fm2_sky.rs` (engine): `FH1_FM2_SKY_ONLY=n`, `FH1_FM2_SKY_TEXDIR` diagnostics, uncompressed RGBA8 DDS loader. `fm2_sky.rs` (importer): PVS flag-1 "runtime" sky textures are looked up in `.stx.bin` (Tsukuba / Silverstone cap = a 1x1 placeholder, skipped). `main.rs`: `FH1_SHOT_PROBE_PX=x,y;..` logs the ground point under screen pixels. Example `stx_list`.
**Found (data, not guessed):** the sky model's materials are `shader_sky_cap` + `shader_sky1..4` (four 90 degree sector photos, alpha = sky coverage, 0 on mountains/ground) + flare. No blend state is stored for them. With the sectors drawn OPAQUE and FM2's post on, Tsukuba's clear sky reads (114,136,159) against Xenia (107,133,156); without the post the photo is (79,101,133). The Xenia sky is the photo pushed through the dark curve (gamma 1.51) and tint, so the earlier alpha-blended "layered" sky was wrong.
**Tested:** 12-track road-band metric (`cmp12.py base12 pa`): sum |ln ratio| 10.94 without the post -> 12.35 with it (post + opaque sky). The roads are not yet right before the post (rubber-streak overlay draws such as model 902 barely show; the importer's road brightness was tuned against Xenia's FINAL pixels, i.e. already post-curved), so the metric moving is expected until roads are re-derived pre-post. Sky and Tsukuba overall look closer.
**Not done / not tested:** make opaque sky + post the default; re-derive road albedo pre-post (BC4 gamma on all roads is the likely first step, needs a re-import); the rubber overlay strength; per-track `t` check; Xenia's large extra cumulus on Tsukuba x1 is NOT in the sector photos (source unknown).
**Follow-up (42):** tried making post + opaque sky the default and re-importing with `FM2_BC4_SRGB_ALL=1`: road metric 12.35 -> 13.45 (not adopted; data re-imported with defaults). Forcing the light curve (`FH1_FM2_POST_T=1`): Tsukuba roads 2.1/2.8 -> 1.18/1.14 but the sum over 12 tracks 21.8 (Sebring, New York, Suzuka far too dark). So the game's per-track curve blend is not reproduced by our frame average (the game's scene buffer is brighter / differently scaled); defaults left as before (post and layered sky behave as in entry 41). Switch set for the best sky: `FH1_FM2_POST=1 FH1_FM2_SKY_LAYERS=0`.

Unexplained; next candidates: the cars cube / SH use in the FM2 car shader, per-track car paint rows.
**(52) car shader (data, not yet used):** FM2 `default.xex` embedded effect @82066f50, techniques body / body_2 / carbon_fiber / DebugSHEnv (dump: `fm2_fxdump data/work_fm2/disc/default.xex body`, output kept in git-ignored data/work/fm2_fx/body.txt). Its vertex shader takes per-car SH coefficients sh0..sh3 R/G/B (c36..c47) and shEnv0..3 (c116..c119), so the car body is lit by spherical-harmonic irradiance + an env cube, not by the engine sun + fill. The pixel-shader side and where the engine would fill the SH from `<SHLighting>` (SHTop / SHBottom / SHDir, SHEnvMapTop / Bottom) is not read yet. This is the faithful route for the dark Sunset / Nurburgring bodies.
**(52) car pixel shader read (git-ignored dump data/work/fm2_fx/body_full.txt):** the body PS takes PaintColor / PaintScale, ColorScale 0.6 / ColorClamp 0.9, SHUpperScale / SHLowerScale / SHTransition (SH band blend by normal height), a Fresnel term (FresnelPower 5, Scale 1, Min 0, Max 1), CubeMultiplier 2.2 on the env cube (static cube when `useStaticCubeMap`), Phong specular (SpecularPower 128 x SpecularAmount 4; second lobe power 10), the track light map (`trackLightmapSampler`, `trackLightMapColour`) and dirt / damage samplers. The per-vertex SH comes from sh0..3 R/G/B uploaded per frame; how the game fills them (from the track SH, or from baked per-location probes) is not traced. Estimate: with SHTop 0.3 / SHBottom 0.09 even a rear (horizontal) normal gets about 0.2 of SH light, so the near-black Xenia bodies on Sunset / Nurburgring need either a lower SH there or the track light-map term to darken the car; not resolved. Porting the whole shader to WGSL is the faithful route (large); not started.
**(52) SH trace (PPC):** the effect-parameter handles sh0R..sh3B are cached in the effect object at +0x26c.. (init 0x82563dxx); the per-draw upload is in the function around 0x8255ec1c, which copies four 16-byte rows each for R / G / B from a per-object lighting block (`*(draw + 0xa0)`, offsets +0, +0x40, +0x80, +0xc0) into the sh parameters via 0x82276758. So the car SH is a per-object light environment, not the track-wide `<SHLighting>`; where that block is filled (a per-location probe sample or a computation from the track SH) is not traced. Helper 0x82555d38 is a matrix decomposition, not the SH.
**(52) car data on the disc:** `Media/cars/ShaderSettings/Normal.xml` and `Metallic.xml` (also Pearl, Copper, ...) hold the FM2 body shader parameters: Normal PaintScale 1.0, Metallic 0.6; ColorScale 0.6 / ColorClamp 0.9; Fresnel Min 0.05 / Max 0.7 / Scale 1.75 / Power 5; CubeMultiplier 12 (the PS multiplies the env cube by it); SH Upper / Lower scale 1, transition 0.5. The importer does not copy them for FM2 cars (the engine reads FH1 car ShaderSettings), so the FM2 body uses FH1 values. A port of the FM2 body shader with these values and the per-object SH block is the faithful car fix; not done.
**(52) pre_00 light entries:** the 0x98-byte "Ambient" entries of each track pre_00 model (type 3, direction (0,-1,0)) carry colours at +0x68 that come in groups of 50 / 100 per value (Sunset 0.36 / 1.0 / 0.24 with alpha 1 or 0.5 pairs, Tsukuba 0.42 / 1.0 / 0.41, Nurburgring 1.0 and 0.0 (100 entries of 0), Sebring 1.0 / 0.0 / 0.01), no position in the entry (positions would be in the model node graph, not decoded). They may be the lookup the car light environment is built from (a track with black entries, Nurburgring / Sebring, vs Xenia: Nurburgring near black, Sebring blue: no clear match). Not decoded further.
**(52) pre_00 structure:** the "Ambient" light entries are indexed (0..~250, by 12-byte index records at data offset 344, class id 0x838fc14) with no position: they are per-index ambient colour levels, not a spatial probe grid. So the car light environment is not read from them by position; the source of the per-object SH block stays untraced.
**(52) more:** no spherical-harmonic basis constants (0.282095 / 0.488603 / ...) exist in the FM2 executable, so the per-object SH block is loaded or copied data, not computed analytically at run time. The body PS also samples the track light map at a position projected by `trackLightmapWVPS` (VS c32..35) and mixes it with `trackLightMapColour` (c171): the car is shadowed by the track light map under it. Not reproduced.

## 2026-10-27 (53): degenerate shader uv rejected (Sunset Infield road grain back)
Sunset model 77 draw 190 (`h_blnd2_ao_lm`, colour texture 252 = grey asphalt grain, wear mask 94): the shader-derived colour uv came out as span u 4612 x v 0, so the grain smeared to a flat olive stripe (Xenia: grey grainy asphalt). `fm2_scenery.rs`: `degenerate_uv` (one axis < 0.001 span while the other > 50) rejects such a coordinate; the draw takes the world-metre planar grain (2.5 m repeat) instead. `FM2_NO_DEGENERATE_UV=1` = old. New diagnostic: `FM2_SHOW_MODEL` prints each candidate's resolved uv span. Tested: Sunset road now grainy grey like Xenia; all twelve tracks re-imported and re-shot: `cmp12.py` sum 8.15 -> 8.07 (Sunset left 0.50 -> 0.63, Mugello right 1.49 -> 1.33, others unchanged). Nurburgring model 118 (`h_blnd2_ao_lm`, family 4aa0eec057a0743d): colour = lerp(tf5, tf4, luma(tf3)); tf4 / tf5 are two small grey grain textures that tile with their own coordinate (resolved uv span 49 x 36), tf3 the weight spanning the draw once. ## 2026-10-27 (65): the game own sun direction path (PPC): the TrackSettings `ShadowLightDirection` is the scene light direction
`0x825220f0` (called from ApplyTrackSettings with the direction of the scene type-1 light, if any) writes the direction into settings +0x550 only while +0x550..0x558 still hold the default (0, -1, 0); the loader fills +0x550 from the XML `ShadowLightDirection` first, so on every track (none has a type-1 light) the effective world light direction is the XML vector. The engine already uses it (`fm2_lighting`, z mirrored), so the four alternative sun candidates tried today (mirrored x, default (1,-1,0), sky disc direction) were off the game path and all fail. The mismatch in shadows / car brightness is therefore in the shading, not in the sun vector.

## 2026-10-27 (76): the car's light block is traced: D3DX spherical-harmonic lights x per-vertex transfer data in the car model (finding + parser field)
**Where it comes from (PPC, `data/work/fm2_xex/text.asm`):** the render-item function at `0x8251f0c8` fills the item's light pointer (`item + 0xa0`, the "per-object block" of entry 52). It builds three sets of 16 floats (R, G, B; order-4 spherical harmonics) with two D3DX library calls: `0x82745f00` = `D3DXSHEvalHemisphereLight(4, up = (0,1,0), top, bottom, R, G, B)` and `0x82745dc8` = `D3DXSHEvalDirectionalLight(4, light direction, colour, R, G, B)`, and adds them. The inputs are the track's `<SHLighting>` (loader at `0x82579130`, struct order verified from the strings: SHTop R,G,B at +0, SHBottom at +0xc, SHDir at +0x18, SHEnvMapTop/Bottom at +0x24/+0x28). A second hemisphere call with SHEnvMapTop/Bottom builds the env block (VS `shEnv0..3`, c116..c119). The light direction is a vector in the render item (+0x80..0x88); the block is rebuilt only when it changes and shared by all cars. `0x82733ad0` (see entry 52) is `D3DXSHRotate` into the car's frame. This is why the executable contains no SH basis constants: they live in the D3DX library.
**What the vertex shader does with it:** it does not evaluate the SH at the normal. It dots four per-vertex vec4 inputs (texcoord1..4, 16 numbers) with the 16 SH coefficients of R, G, B and Env (`o6.xyz` = lit colour, `o6.w` = env visibility). That is precomputed radiance transfer (PRT): the self-shadowing of the body is baked into the model.
**Where the 16 numbers are in the file:** the 60-byte carbin vertex has them in its undecoded tail, bytes 28..60 of the vertex = 16 big-endian signed 16-bit values (VERIFIED on the Skyline GT-R V-spec II, 66k vertices): the first is about 16400 (0.50) on open surfaces; the next three follow -y, +z, -x of the surface direction (up: -15800 on the y one; +z side: +17000 on the z one; +x: -17100 on the x one), i.e. the D3DX real SH order l=0, l=1 (y, z, x with D3DX signs), then l=2, l=3. The first 12 tail bytes hold the normal / tangent frame in an encoding not decoded (the first short follows the z of the normal, 0.98 correlation); not needed, normals come from the triangles.
**Changed:** `fh1-formats/src/carbin_fm2.rs`: `Vertex::tail` (bytes 16..60, undecoded, no behaviour change); new example `carbin_fm2_tail` (writes a CSV of vertex, normal, tail bytes for analysis, to a path you give it).
**Not done yet:** using the transfer data in the engine's car paint.

## 2026-10-27 (81): the car's own light (PRT + SH) in the engine, opt-in `FH1_FM2_CAR_PRT=1` (step 1)
**Built (from entry 76):** importer `fm2_cars.rs` writes `cars/<id>/prt.bin` (b"PRT1", per body part its 16 transfer numbers per vertex; 310 cars re-imported, the Skyline's is 0.8 MB); `fh1-remaster/src/car_prt.rs` (new): loads it, gives each car part its own mesh copy, and every time the car turns against the sun writes per vertex a lit colour (COLOR_0 = transfer . (hemisphere SH from `SHTop` / `SHBottom` + one directional SH from `SHDir`, D3DX basis), stored / (32768 x sqrt(pi)) = light units) plus UV_1 = (sun visibility from the transfer, environment visibility from `SHEnvMapTop/Bottom`). `car_paint.rs`: with `prt` > 0 the paint's diffuse is paint x the vertex light x `FH1_FM2_CAR_PRT_GAIN` (default 1; the overall level is INFERRED) with Bevy's diffuse removed (only its specular stays), the whole colour is multiplied by `lerp(ShadowColor^2.2, 1, sun visibility)` (the body shader's `lerp(ShadowColour, 1, visibility)`, `FH1_FM2_CAR_SHADE=0` off), the cube reflection gets the environment visibility and the entry 80 multiplier. `main.rs` reads `<SHLighting>`; `FH1_FM2_PRT_SKY=1` takes the sun direction from the sky disc instead of the XML vector. 4 unit tests (SH normalisation, file round trip).
**Measured (all twelve, three body patches (trunk lid, roof, rear panel) in display values, mean |ours - Xenia| over 12 x 3 patches, `data/work/carscore2.py`; lower is better):** baseline 46.6; PRT with XML sun 47.8 (`prt5`); PRT with sky-disc sun 46.6 (`prt6`); the earlier variant without the transfer-based visibility 48.4 / 47.9. Whole-car region (x 450..840, y 420..710) mean |diff|: baseline 43.1, `prt5` 45.3, `prt6` 47.7 (ours brighter than Xenia by 13.4 baseline, 22 with PRT: the paint gain is not calibrated). By eye (contact sheet Xenia / baseline / PRT, six tracks): PRT is closer on Road Atlanta, Nurburgring, Silverstone (deep navy instead of the baseline's grey-blue; Silverstone's sky reflection), the same on Tsukuba, worse on Maple Valley (bright blue and white where Xenia is black) and Sunset Infield (bright where Xenia is black). Rear panel patch (Xenia in brackets): New York (7,5,16) [8,7,15], Mugello (32,49,127) [28,51,126], Tsukuba (22,37,97) [31,44,82], Nurburgring (14,19,28) [3,4,10] against the baseline's (15,12,26), (37,46,104), (22,31,72), (21,35,49).
**Verdict:** the light block's source is found and the game's lighting is rebuilt from it, but the result is only neutral against the existing inferred look (46.6 vs 46.6 or 47.8), better on about half the circuits and worse on the rest, so it stays OFF by default. What is missing is whatever makes Xenia's cars on Road Atlanta / Maple Valley / New York / Laguna Seca / Sunset near black: the body shader's own shadow (a variance shadow map of the track and car, `doselfshadow`, the sun it reads is not known), not the SH term. Not tested: AI cars (the update recomputes up to 66k vertices per car whenever it turns 2.5 degrees; the CPU cost with many cars is not measured); performance in general.

(1) The GPU figure path (`figures_gpu.rs`) draws the photographers, marshals and pit workers (classes 3 to 5) as nothing; the CPU path draws them correctly (a teleport beside the balcony with `FH1_CROWD_GPU_FIGURES=0` showed the two marshals, two photographers and the wall row exactly as in Xenia). Root cause not found (meshes, textures and animations check out: `crates/fh1-formats/examples/skin_probe.rs`, new); on FM2 maps classes 3 to 5 now use the CPU path (`crowd.rs spawn_figure`), about 10 to 55 figures. (2) The figure count: `<Crowds lod0..lod3>` (5 / 20 / 50 / 125) read as counts capped the 3D figures at 200 (cut 33 to 40 m); on FM2 maps they are now read as distances (a figure's skinbin LOD by its distance: <5 m LOD 0, <20 LOD 1, <50 LOD 2, <125 LOD 3, beyond that the cards), with a cap of 600 figures (`FH1_CROWD_FIG_CAP`; `FH1_CROWD_LOD_DIST=0` = the old count reading). Whether the numbers mean distances is INFERRED (it makes Xenia's picture, not a fact from the file). Measured, one shot each, frame time (HUD): Mugello 7.0 ms (200 figures) -> 7.1 (600) -> 7.6 (1884), Sunset 7.3 -> 7.2 (600) -> 7.8 (2000), New York 7.2 -> 7.1 (600). By eye Mugello, New York and Sunset match Xenia's shots (balcony and wall row now there on Mugello). Not tested: a full lap, other circuits' staff, GPU-memory growth, anything below 512 GPU slots (figures past slot 512 take the CPU path).
**(86) GPU figure bug FOUND and fixed (replaces the CPU workaround for classes 3 to 5 above):** the figures' skinning-matrix buffer (`crowd_mats`, `figures_gpu.rs`) started one vec4 long and grew every time a new clip was baked; a growing `ShaderBuffer` is re-created on the GPU and a material created before the growth kept the old, too-short buffer, so its figures read zeros and collapsed (they showed as black dots, or not at all). Found by experiment (NY, class 1 only): the same figures with every texture swapped for one single texture vanished, swapped for 27 mismatched textures they showed, the marshal mesh with ordinary textures showed, the idle clips on ordinary figures showed; the clip data itself is clean (`crates/fh1-formats/examples/anim_probe.rs`, new). Fix: the buffer is allocated at 16 MB up front (`FH1_CROWD_MATS_MB`) and rewritten in place, so it never re-creates. Verified by eye: staff on the GPU path draw on Mugello (balcony people, wall row), the single-texture test draws, New York and Sunset unchanged. The CPU workaround for classes 3 to 5 is removed. Frame time (HUD, two runs each): New York 7.1 / 7.1, Sunset 7.4 / 7.4, Mugello 7.1; some single runs read 60 FPS / 16 ms on any build (seen before this change too, cause not looked into). Cost: 16 MB of GPU memory, rewritten whenever a new clip is baked. Not tested: Colorado (the same code; its figures may have been silently affected by the same bug), a full lap, memory if more than 16 MB of clips are ever baked (the buffer then grows as before).
**(86) Colorado check after the GPU buffer change:** the home spot (Dak's Garage) draws the same crowd with the old unpadded buffer (`FH1_CROWD_MATS_MB=1`) and the new 16 MB one by eye (200 figures loaded within 60 m, 15681 spectators, 247 walker paths); frame time 9.7 ms old / 9.8 ms new (HUD, one run each). Not tested: Colorado's walkers close up, other spots on the map, a long drive.

## 2026-10-29 (87): New York's "Welcome to Forza Motorsport 2 - Fuel Your Fantasy" marquee, and the cut-out rule
**Cause:** the marquee (13 m x 1 m, two-sided quads about 20 m ahead of the start and 5.5 m up, tile `-2_-2`, texture `00000209.dds`, 512 x 32) existed in the tiles but drew as nothing. The importer's cut-out rule (`fm2_scenery.rs cutout_texture`: 3 to 97 % of the texels with alpha under 128) called it a cut-out, but its alpha only runs 21..168: that is another map (glow / gloss), not opacity, and the engine's alpha test at 0.5 deleted the picture. **Fix:** a texture counts as a cut-out only if its alpha has (nearly) clear and (nearly) opaque texels (min <= 64 and max >= 192); `FM2_OLD_CUTOUT=1` = the old rule. All 32 maps re-imported and the remaster tables rebuilt (`import-fm2 data/work_fm2/disc --only maps`, `fh1setup data/work/disc --only remaster --force`); New York 396 cut-out textures after (the Python count of the old rule on the same folder: 785).
**Checked:** by eye New York now shows the marquee at the Xenia position with the same text (ours: dimmer, brown band where Xenia has black with bright orange, i.e. Xenia draws it as a lit LED sign: not done); the other eleven circuits look unchanged by eye (Tsukuba, Sunset, Mugello, Maple Valley, Nurburgring, Silverstone, Suzuka, Road Atlanta); road bands 6.48 (the rule alone, no crowd; baseline 6.34 to 6.43). Not checked: the 225 other New York textures the rule flipped from cut-out to opaque (facade glass, signs such as the News Corporation panel) one by one.
**Side finding / fix:** re-importing the maps deletes each map's folder, `crowd/` included (the crowds vanished after the re-import); the maps import now also writes the crowd placement (`fm2.rs`). Road bands with the crowd back read 7.46 (the crowd's people reach the edge of the measured bands, so this is not comparable with 6.48).
**Test:** `$env:FH1_OWNERSHIP="0"; .\target\release\fh1-engine.exe --track fm2/newyork_01`: an orange "Welcome to Forza Motorsport 2 - Fuel Your Fantasy" sign under the NISSAN banner.
**(87) the marquee lit like Xenia's:** read the game's pixel shader for the marquee (new debug `FM2_DUMP_PS=<texture id hex>` prints each draw's disassembly, `FM2_PS_SIG=<hash>` lists a shader family's draws): `rgb * (k + (1 - k) * c104.w) * light` with k from the texture alpha, light = the frame's ambient + sun (global constants c100 to c102, not set by the draw). Two pixel-shader hashes (`3d69a1a8dce9ca9e`, `8ae1a07996db6f64`, differing only in which constant register holds which value) draw New York's marquee strip (0x209), the dome lamps (0x5ea), the Forza strips (0x432) and two other boards (0x12, 0x677); no other of the 32 maps uses them (10 draws in all). Those draws now carry `"led": true` in `materials.json` and the remaster record is `Unlit` without the lightmap light, albedo x `FH1_FM2_LED_GAIN` (1.3, INFERRED from the Xenia shot) (`fm2_scenery.rs LED_SIGS`, `remaster.rs record`, `FM2_NO_LED=1` = off). Measured on the marquee region (brightest 300 px / darkest 1500 px), Xenia text (236,188,125), band (6,2,1); before ours (153,125,109) / (20,13,12), now (243,186,109) / (26,19,15). By eye the sign reads like Xenia's. Not tested: the dome lamps and strips by eye (they are tiny in the start view), night.
**Test:** `.\target\debug\fh1setup.exe import-fm2 data\work_fm2\disc --only maps` and `.\target\debug\fh1setup.exe data\work\disc --only remaster --force` once, then `$env:FH1_OWNERSHIP="0"; .\target\release\fh1-engine.exe --track fm2/newyork_01`: a bright amber sign on black under the NISSAN banner.

## 2026-10-29 (88): Nurburgring's pale "frosted" spruce trees (dead end, nothing changed in the result)
**Correction:** the pale blue tree-like object I filed under Maple Valley is on **Nurburgring** (x7): I read the rows of my own comparison sheet one off. Compared with Xenia's shot, Nurburgring's trees differ in more than that object: Xenia has dense dark-green canopies, ours has pale bare trunks, sparse leaf clusters, and some evergreens (the spruce sheets `0x20`, `0xb2`, `0xb3`, materials `h_diff_opac_1(_lm)`) drawn pale cyan, opaque, like frost.
**Found:** the leaf / branch textures (`0x7`, `0x9`, `0xb`, `0x10`, `0x28`, `0x123` ...) are black silhouettes with alpha: RGB is the real colour, the pixel shader (`FM2_DUMP_PS=10`) is `texture.rgb * 2 * lerp(c184, c185, lightmap)`, alpha = texture alpha x an interpolator, then fog: nothing hides a green. The spruce materials bind the 1 x 1 texture `33` (value 33) as their "lightmap" (slot 7), the maples a real 32 x 32 tile.
**Ruled out (by experiment, Nurburgring re-imported each time, look unchanged):** the black key (`FM2_NO_KEY=1`: canopies turn into black boxes, the spruces stay pale), the new cut-out rule (`FM2_OLD_CUTOUT=1`), the sun sheen on cut-outs (a `cls != CLASS_CUTOUT` guard changed nothing; removed again). The sprite composer is not involved (one sprite made, `FM2_DEBUG` prints `sprite rejected` patterns now). **Not tried:** the engine's lightmap sampling of a 1 x 1 tile (suspect: it may read white), the tree materials' vertex colours, the two-sided / mip alpha of the cards.
**Added for debugging (importer):** `FM2_DEBUG` prints the rejected sprite patterns; the earlier `FM2_DUMP_PS` / `FM2_PS_SIG`. Stopped after two attempts as AGENTS.md says.
**(88) FIXED, Nurburgring's pale spruces (replaces the "dead end" above):** a bisection (hiding groups of cut-out materials in the remaster table, counting pale pixels in a close-up) found one texture, `0x19f`; it is the first of two textures on its draw, and the pixel shader (`FM2_DUMP_PS=19f`) fetches only its alpha (`tfetch2D r3.w___, ..., tf0`) and the colour from the second (`0x1a0`, dark green: mean (31,40,24) against the first's pale (66,96,97)). The importer took the first as the colour. **Fix (`fm2_scenery.rs alpha_only_items`):** a texture the shader fetches only the alpha of is not a colour candidate when another colour exists; `FM2_ALPHA_ONLY_COLOUR=1` = the old rule. All maps re-imported, remaster rebuilt. The 1 x 1 lightmap suspect was tested and is innocent (removing tile 33 from its 22 materials turned the distant pines orange-brown, so it is read correctly). By eye the frosted spruces are dark green now; Nurburgring's trees overall are still thinner and lighter than Xenia's dense dark canopies (not done). Road bands 7.50 (7.46 before; crowd in the bands, noise about 0.1). Not checked: every other map's trees one by one (twelve circuits looked at as a sheet, nothing obviously worse). ## 2026-10-29 (90): terrain / road blend draws baked from their own pixel shaders (Silverstone's curb outline)
**What:** the multi-layer blend pixel-shader family (docs/DESIGN.md, 2026-10-29) is now run on the CPU: `fm2_scenery.rs bake_blend` rasterises every triangle of a draw in its lightmap uv square (6 texels per metre, 16 to 256 per side, `FM2_BAKE_PX_PER_M`), interpolates each sampler's resolved uv, runs the draw's pixel shader (`fh1-shaders/src/ps_cpu.rs`, refactored: `shader_eval_at` runs it at any set of points, `shader_eval_verts` is the old per-vertex entry, now also returns the output alpha) with neutral lighting and the real textures bound, and writes the result (sRGB-coded, alpha = the shader's opacity) as one texture per draw (`0xFFFF_9800..`). The draw then has one plain colour candidate on the lightmap uv and its lightmap: texels with alpha < 0.5 make it a cut-out. Default on for four shader families (`FM2_BAKE_SIGS`, `FM2_NO_BAKE_BLEND=1` = off): `fadd5e0531745ff7` and `9e00faa9a03af266` (Silverstone's curb / grass-edge draws, texture `0x109`; found by hiding material groups until the outline went, then `FM2_DUMP_PS=109`) and `8958d8764fba0e07`, `97a83cd67d54938a` (Road Atlanta's road paint: yellow and white lines, kerb bands; the baked textures show them as lines on black, so alpha matters).
**Result, Silverstone (by eye, one shot):** the white-and-black outline round the grass patch is gone; the grass has a clean (slightly jagged, cut-out) edge like Xenia's, which has no curb line there. 74 draws baked, 16 could not run (unsupported instructions; they keep the old path). **Road Atlanta's yellow / green boxes at the wall base are NOT these draws** (baking the family did not change them); they stay open.
**Not tested:** the effect on the other 11 circuits (all re-imported with the default on, shots follow), Road Atlanta's paint lines by eye, the 16 / 4 draws that could not run, texel density of long strips (256 per side may be coarse for 100 m strips).
**(90) all maps re-imported with the bake on:** baked draws per map: Silverstone 74 (+16 could not run), Road Atlanta 223 (+4), two others 15 and 3, eight maps 0 (the four families occur on three or four circuits only). Road bands 7.47 (7.50 before: no change, noise about 0.1). By eye (Silverstone, Road Atlanta, Tsukuba, Suzuka against Xenia) nothing got worse; Silverstone's grass-edge outline stays gone. Still open on those shots: Silverstone has another white / black curb arc at the far left of the road (a different draw family, not found), Road Atlanta's wall-base boxes, Suzuka's red line across the start (also in the earlier shots), the skies, Nurburgring's canopies.
**(90) follow-up, the 16 draws that "could not run":** they ran fine but covered no texel: thin curb strips lie between the centres of a 16 to 256 texel square taken over the whole lightmap uv range. The bake now covers each draw's own lightmap-uv bounding box (`bake_blend` returns the normalised uvs that address it). Silverstone: 90 of 90 draws bake; the far-left white / black curb arc is gone too, the grass edge is smoother (by eye, one shot). Remaining difference at that spot: Xenia's grass patch is a drier tan, ours green (a colour treatment of the grass blend, not touched). Debug: `FM2_DEBUG=1` prints `bake failed:` and `shader ... unsupported:` lines. All maps re-imported, remaster rebuilt.
**(90) Tsukuba start area re-checked by eye after the bake (Xenia x1 against ours):** the yellow chevron and the start-line dashes are both present now; ours is a little thinner / broken (the chevron has a gap) and darker than Xenia's, the pylon is not in the start view. Not pursued further.
**(90) Road Atlanta wall-base boxes, one more attempt:** dumped the draws under the wall (`FM2_NEAR`): other shader families (`f2936bdaec6f20df`, `ef96ddbdada8d13a`, `9958d0e45436df7b`) with straw texture `0x10c`/lightmap; baking those two as well (1838 draws, 249 could not run) did not remove the boxes, so the default set is unchanged. Still open, with the skies, Nurburgring canopies and Suzuka's red start line. Two attempts made; stopping per AGENTS.md.
**(90) Suzuka's red line and the sky ratio, checked:** Suzuka's red line across the start is in Xenia's shot too (thin, dark red); ours is the same line but brighter red: not a defect, closed. Sky: the ratio Xenia / ours of the mean brightness of a sky band, per circuit (`data/work/skyratio.py`), against each track's `TrackSettings.xml` `skyGain`: Tsukuba 0.40 -> 1.43, Sunset 0.45 -> 0.93, Silverstone 0 -> 0.93, Sebring 0.30 -> 0.94, Road Atlanta 0.33 -> 0.62, Nurburgring 0.20 -> 1.21, New York 2.25 -> 0.48, Mugello 0 -> 1.25, Maple Valley 0.10 -> 0.48, Laguna 0.08 -> 0.66, Suzuka 0.30 -> 1.20. ## 2026-10-29 (92): Road Atlanta's wall-base boxes FIXED: sparse grass-tuft cards were drawn opaque
**Cause (found by listing the draws round the wall base with `FM2_NEAR`, then viewing their textures):** the boxes are grass tufts, `h_diff_opac` cards with 64 x 128 textures (`0x3`, `0x4`, `0x7`) that are 98 to 99.8 % clear (a few strands, alpha up to 143 at most). `cutout_texture` (fm2_scenery.rs) accepted 3 to 97 % transparent only, and my entry-87 range rule (alpha must reach 192) rejected `0x7`; they stayed opaque and drew as full rectangles in grass / straw colours. All my record / texture bisections missed it because the boxes come from dozens of materials. **Fix:** a texture with at least one solid (alpha >= 128) texel and 3 %+ clear texels is a cut-out whatever the upper bound (`FM2_OLD_CUTOUT_SPARSE=1` = old upper limit), and the range rule now needs only max alpha >= 128 and min <= 64 (`FM2_OLD_CUTOUT=1` = the old 3 to 97 % rule alone). 328 cut-out textures on Road Atlanta (was 321).
**Checked:** Road Atlanta by eye: the boxes are gone, the wall base matches Xenia's. All 32 maps re-imported and the remaster rebuilt; twelve circuits re-shot (`sp`): road bands 6.57 (6.75 before), no circuit looks worse by eye (Nurburgring, Maple Valley, Suzuka, Tsukuba, Mugello viewed; Suzuka's fence gained leaf growth like Xenia's). Pixel differences against the previous shots (|diff| > 90): Road Atlanta 95k (the boxes), Maple Valley 26k, Suzuka 24k, Sunset 22k, the rest under 16k.
**Test:** `$env:FH1_OWNERSHIP="0"; .\target\release\fh1-engine.exe --track fm2/roadatlanta_00`: no yellow / green rectangles along the wall.
**(92) Nurburgring canopies, fifth look (after the sparse-cut-out fix, still open):** in the re-shot, the big round tree Xenia shows at screen x about 490 is still a bare pole with a few leaf specks in ours (same tree: same screen position). Its canopy draw is not identified: the draws I listed round the first trees (bark + a 57-triangle leaf card sheet) are present and drawn, and the 154 rejected multi-colour sprite patterns in `FM2_DEBUG` output (`CCCCMCC`, 512 / 1024 textures) look like terrain blends, not trees. Locating that tree's world position from the screen (bearing from the spawn along its facing) with `FM2_NEAR` found nothing at 90 / 140 / 200 m, so my position estimate is off. Not fixed.

## 2026-10-29 (93): far cut-outs keep their coverage (Nurburgring's thin canopies)
**Cause:** the big round tree Xenia shows at screen x about 490 on Nurburgring was a bare pole with leaf specks in ours although its leaf cards are drawn: its leaf sheet is about 18 % opaque, and at distance the mip-averaged alpha falls under the 0.5 alpha test, so most leaves are discarded (the game's alpha-to-coverage keeps the coverage). Lowering the cutoff globally (entry 91) showed this (0.12 gave a real canopy) but also thickened near leaves. **Fix (`fh1-remaster/src/material.rs`, cut-out branch of the shader):** the cutoff falls with distance from the camera, x 1.0 up to 15 m to x `FH1_RM_CUTOUT_FAR` (default 0.3, INFERRED: 0.12 / 0.5 looked right at about 150 m) from 80 m on, cut-outs only (`FH1_RM_CUTOUT_FAR=1` = off).

The remaining canopy difference is therefore the scene's haze (a global look difference seen on every circuit's percentiles, MODLOG 84), not a tree problem.

So there is no night scenery to differ, and the two-sided / baked / cut-out changes cannot misbehave at night. Closed as not applicable.
**(94) sky grading, last check:** the game's own final combine (`FH1_FM2_POST`, per-track tone curves from `ToneMappingLevels`) is already on by default (`main.rs`: on unless `=0`), so the sky ratios measured with the gain table off (Tsukuba 1.55, Sunset 1.06, Silverstone 0.91, Sebring 0.96 ...) are the residual AFTER the game's own combine; re-shooting with it explicitly on and the gain off reproduces them. The residual cannot be explained from the files read so far (`skyGain`, the tone curves, the sky draws' constants c1 = 2.0 on every track), so the per-circuit gain table stays as the only closure of it: INFERRED, documented, overridable (`FH1_FM2_SKY_GAIN=1`).
**(94) sky check completed for all nine circuits (gain off, game's combine on, `pc`):** Tsukuba 1.55, Sunset 1.06, Silverstone 0.91, Sebring 0.96, Nurburgring 1.25, Mugello 1.40, Maple Valley 0.85, Laguna 0.85, Suzuka 1.20: the same residuals as before the gain table (entry 91), so the gain table, not the combine, is what closes them (with it: 0.87 to 1.17).
**(94) sky shader read attempt:** added `FM2_DUMP_SKY_PS=1` (importer) to print the sky model's pixel shaders; on Mugello the shader resolver finds none for any of the 8 sky draws ("no pixel shader resolved"): the sky model's shaders are not bound the way the scenery models' are, so the game's sky shader (the place a per-track sky grade would live) is still unread. That is the concrete next lead for the sky: locate and disassemble `shader_sky_cap` / `shader_sky1..4`.
**(94) the game's sky shader, partly read (`FM2_DUMP_SKY_PS=1`, importer, new `fm2_uv::dump_shaders`):** the sky model's draws bind pixel-shader containers in the model's `.data` section (`ps_container`): draw 0 (the cap / sun disc, `c_constant0`, `c_constant1` = (2,2,2,2)) and draws 1 to 4 / 5 to 6 (the sector panoramas): constants **`c_skyMultiplier` (register c188)**, **`c_globalSpecularLevel` (c104)** and `s_sampler0`. So the game DOES grade the sky with a per-frame multiplier, `c188`, set by its code, not stored in `TrackSettings.xml` (only `skyGain` is there, and it does not predict the residuals: skyGain 0 gives 0.91 on Silverstone and 1.40 on Mugello). The containers' microcode is zeroed in the file (the code is not in the model: the tail holds only vertex shaders), so the exact formula (`tex * c188 [* ...]`) is not read, and the value of c188 would have to be found in the game's code (default.xex, where something writes constant 188 from the track / time-of-day tables). This explains why a fitted per-circuit multiplier (the gain table) can stand in for it, and names the one place the real value lives. Debug: `crates/fh1-shaders/src/fm2.rs disassemble` is now `pub`.
**(94) c_skyMultiplier traced in the game's code (default.xex image, `data/work/fm2_xex/text.asm`):** the XML loader reads `skyGain` (string at 0x82045778, code 0x825252fc) into field `0x684` of the lighting object (`stfs f0, 0x684(r30)` at 0x82525350); the per-frame update at 0x8252a7e8 copies that field into the render-constant block at offset 0xf50 (`lfs f0, 0x684(r31)` / `stfs f0, 0xf50(r11)`), and the pass setup at 0x82507b58 uploads that block's vec4 as shader constant **188** (`li r4, 0xbc`, block at obj + 0xf50). So **`c_skyMultiplier` = `skyGain`** (first component) from `TrackSettings.xml`: the multiplier is game data and is already the same `skyGain` the engine reads (`TimeGain.y`, lighting.rs). What is NOT known is the sky pixel shader's formula using it: its microcode is not in the model's `.data` containers (zeroed) nor in the track shader files nor the exe, and a plain `tex * skyGain` is impossible (Mugello and Silverstone have skyGain 0 and visible skies). The correlation of the measured Xenia / ours sky ratio with skyGain is 0.32 over the nine circuits, so skyGain alone does not close the residuals; the per-circuit gain table stays the closure (inferred). Debug: `FM2_DUMP_SKY_PS`.
**(94) THE GAME'S SKY PIXEL SHADER, READ (brute-force scan of the sky model's `.gpu` tail: `FM2_DUMP_SKY_PS=1` now also lists pixel-looking blocks; the containers in `.data` only carry names, the microcode sits in the tail at gpu+0x28e0):**
`r2 = tex(uv); A = r2.a * c188.x + c255.x; r1 = A * r2.rgb; out.rgb = r1 + (r2.rgb - r1) * c104.w; out.a = r2.a * c104.w + c255.x`, i.e. `out.rgb = tex.rgb * (A + (1 - A) * c104.w)`. c188.x = `skyGain` (traced above) and **c104 = `GlobalSpecularLevel` (r, g, b, a): `.w` is the alpha component, 1.0 on every track**, so the bracket is 1 and **the game's sky shader returns the texture colour unchanged on every circuit: there is no per-track sky multiplier in it.** (c255.x is 0 or 1 for the alpha term only; the colour does not depend on it.) What the engine draws (texture x 1, then the game's final combine) is therefore the game's own sky up to that combine. The per-track residuals between Xenia and ours (Tsukuba 1.55 ... Maple Valley 0.85) must come from a later stage that varies with the scene: the game's adaptive exposure (`ToneMapeDelay` 0.95, `TrackLumLimits` min / max, `DetailBlurScale` in `TrackSettings.xml`; the engine's combine is a fixed curve per track), not from the sky itself. The per-circuit gain table is thus a stand-in for the game's per-frame luminance adaptation, which is not modelled (INFERRED closure stays; the real mechanism is named).
**(94) adaptive-exposure lead tested:** across the nine circuits the Xenia / ours sky ratio correlates 0.78 with `TrackLumLimits` max (Tsukuba 380 -> 1.55, Nurburgring 350 -> 1.25, Suzuka 310 -> 1.20, Mugello 296 -> 1.40, Maple Valley 280 -> 0.85, Sebring 270 -> 0.96, Silverstone 240 -> 0.91, Laguna 215 -> 0.85, Sunset 211 -> 1.06), against 0.32 for skyGain, -0.42 for LumLimits min, 0.24 / 0.62 / -0.52 for the light curve's white point / gamma and the dark curve's white point. A power law ratio = 0.0114 x max^0.81 explains part of it (rms ln error 0.138 against 0.225 for no sky gain; per circuit off by 0.82 (Sunset, Mugello) to 1.29 (Maple Valley)), so the game's luminance limits are the right family of cause (adaptive exposure maps the scene's luminance into min..max) but the fit is no better than the per-circuit table and is not adopted: the real mechanism (the exposure update in the exe, `ToneMapeDelay`, scene luminance of the frame) is not modelled. The per-circuit table stays.
**(94) the combine's luminance blend `t` tested against the sky (`FH1_FM2_POST_T`, sky gain off, nine circuits; `data/work/skyratio2.py`):** sky patch ratio Xenia / ours with `t` forced to 0 (the dark curve) / forced to 1 (light curve) / computed from the frame: Tsukuba 1.22 / 1.55 / 1.55, Sunset 1.04 / 1.18 / 1.06, Silverstone 0.91 / 0.86 / 0.91, Sebring 0.96 / 1.39 / 0.96, Nurburgring 1.21 / 1.38 / 1.25, Mugello 1.01 / 1.96 / 1.40, Maple Valley 0.81 / 1.36 / 0.85, Laguna 0.85 / 0.85 / 0.85, Suzuka 1.20 / 1.33 / 1.20 (sum |ln| 1.14 / 2.84 / 1.72; the per-circuit gain table: 0.64). So the sky matches Xenia best on the DARK curve and the engine's computed `t` is too high on Tsukuba and Mugello (the circuits whose residuals were largest), i.e. the frame-luminance estimate feeding `t` (post.rs, "estimated from a grid of samples of the frame") is the likely source of the residuals: the game averages the HDR scene, ours a display-coded grid. But `t` = 0 everywhere wrecks the roads (road bands 11.32 against 6.46), so the fix is a better luminance estimate, not a constant; not done. The sky gain table remains the working closure.
**(94) the luminance estimate checked against the game's own downsample:** the game's adaptation chain (`AveragedDownSample`, `DownSample16X`, `data/work/fm2_fx`) is a plain mip-chain average of the gamma-coded frame (one `tfetch2D UseRegisterLOD`, output copied), which is what `post.rs` does with its 8 x 6 grid; there is no log-average or HDR weighting to copy. So `t` is computed like the game's, and Tsukuba's / Mugello's higher `t` follows from OUR frame being brighter than Xenia's (sky and darks: the global "ours lighter in the darks" difference of entry 84), not from a different estimator. The sky gain table therefore compensates for the same global brightness difference; closing that for real means matching the scene brightness itself, which no game-data lead explains yet. No code change.

## 2026-10-29 (97): FM6 Nissan IDx, step 1: the `burG` container parser
New `crates/fh1-formats/src/grub.rs` (reads only the wrapper of FM6's `.modelbin` / `.swatchbin` / `.materialbin`; chunk contents are NOT decoded yet), probe examples `grub_probe.rs` (checks every such file under a folder) and `zip_extract.rs` (unpacks a Forza zip). Plan: docs/DESIGN.md "FM6 IDx".
**Layout found (from the bytes, then checked):** header = `burG`, u16 version 0x0101, data start, file size, chunk count; a 24-byte entry per chunk (reversed 4-byte tag, version, metadata offset, data offset, size, size again); metadata blocks between the table and the data; data blocks back to back, 4-aligned.
**Check (`grub_probe data/work/fm6_idx`, IDx unpacked from `NIS_IDXNismo_15.zip`):** 159 of 159 files fit the rules (first data offset = data start; each chunk starts where the previous padded one ended; both size fields equal; file ends at the last block or its padding). First run: 91 of 159, because I wrongly required padding after the last block (swatchbins end exactly, materialbins pad): fixed in the rule, not in the data.
**Chunks seen (count, versions):** `Mesh` 1423 (0x20801), `MatI` 311 (0x20001), `VerB` 331 and `MBuf` 101 (vertex bytes, 0x10001), `IndB` 81 (index bytes), `VLay` 266 (vertex layouts, 0x10101), `Skel` 82, `Modl` 82, `Mrph` 39, `TXCB` 68 (texture pixels, 56.6 MB, 0x10002), `MATL` 9, `MTPR` 9. Tag meanings other than the obvious are guesses until step 2 / 3 decode them.
**Not tested:** other FM6 cars, tracks, or files outside the IDx. Nothing touches the engine yet; nothing is committed.

## 2026-10-29 (99): FM6 IDx step 2, second round: mip/tile arithmetic found, layout still unsolved (no engine change)
Facts and what was tried are in STATUS.md "BLOCKED", "Second round". Found: mips are stored largest first and each is padded to whole 8x8-block micro tiles (checked by byte arithmetic on two textures); within a tile a Morton order scores best, but the whole-image score disagrees between two textures, so the layout is not confirmed. The game executable has no useful tiling strings; `xg_x.dll` (in the FM6 EmbeddedXvd folder) is the next place to look. Added `x=<positions>` mode to `swatch_probe`. Still not tested: a correct decode of any texture. Recommendation written: do step 3 (meshes) with grey materials first.

## 2026-10-29 (100): FM6 IDx step 2, third round: the layout is AMD AddrLib (xg_x.dll), format flags corrected; no picture yet
Found that the game's texture layout comes from Microsoft's `xg_x.dll` / AMD AddrLib (1D / 2D thin tile modes), listed by name in the dll. Re-tabulated bytes per pixel against the four flag bytes (STATUS.md "Third round"): the first flag byte alone does not give the format, so the earlier BC1 / BC4 / BC5 mapping is unproven. A per-mip padding model fits 32 of 68 files only. No decoder written; nothing in the engine changed. Not tested: any correct texture decode; reading xg_x.dll's code.

## 2026-10-29 (101): FM6 IDx step 2, fourth round: small mips are dense; 2e textures are BC7; big-mip tile order still unsolved
Details in STATUS.md "Fourth round". Firm: the smallest mips fill slots 0..N-1 of their 512-byte slot in order (3 textures); the 2e-flag 1 byte per pixel textures are BC7 (block mode statistics); the AO maps' format is unknown. Partial: tile order of an 8-tile mip fits a 3-bit XOR rule; larger mips give no consistent rule; "linear inside an 8x8 tile" is unconfirmed (mip 3 decodes jagged). Added `l<N>` mode to `swatch_probe` (linear inside tiles). Helper scripts in `data/work` (smallmips, smallview, tileperm, tilegf2). No decoder, nothing in the engine, nothing committed.

## 2026-10-29 (102): FM6 IDx step 2, fifth round: in-tile order proven (Morton), small-mip tile order solved, big-mip macro tiling still open
Read the layout code in `xg_x.dll` with capstone: thin micro tiles are Morton `x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2`, 8x8 elements (4x4 blocks) per tile. Confirmed on the data by mip-to-mip consistency (mismatch 1.8 vs 12.8, 4.9 vs 68.6, 3.4 vs 105 against row-by-row). Mips up to 4x4 tiles use row-by-row tile order (checked on two textures). Mips of 8x8 tiles and more use a macro-tiled order I could not fit (flat or empty tiles give too few confident matches). Earlier readings "linear inside the tile" (rounds 2 and 4) are superseded. Helper scripts in `data/work` (xgdis, mortonchain, tilematch, tilesolve, tileorder, bu2, chain). No decoder written yet; nothing in the engine; nothing committed.

## 2026-10-29 (103): FM6 IDx step 2 SOLVED: `.swatchbin` decoder (Xbox One GPU tiling) in `fh1-formats`
New `crates/fh1-formats/src/swatch.rs`: header, format from the flag bytes (BC1, BC4, BC5, BC7, 8888), mip sizes (every mip padded to whole 8x8-element tiles in both directions), and the AMD GCN address rule (1D thin with Morton tiles below 64x32 elements; 2D thin macro tiles above: 8 pipes, 4 banks, `p0 = x3^y3^x4, p1 = x4^y4, p2 = x5^y5, b0 = y4^y5^x6, b1 = y3^x7`). Two unit tests (the layout is a permutation; first tile is Morton). New example `swatch_dump` writes PNGs.
**How found and checked:** STATUS.md "Sixth round": per-block matching against the next smaller mip plus an exact XOR fit, then generalised; each mip, shrunk 2x, matches the next with mismatch 0.06 to 1.7 on BC1, BC4, BC5 and 8888 textures up to 1024x1024 (wrong layouts: 5 to 113).
**Result on the IDx:** 37 of 68 textures decode; the BC7 interior atlas and the livery look right by eye. Not decoded: 27 AO maps and 2 `Rear_*` maps (format unknown), ProxyLOD (probably 16-bit float), 2 grill maps and 1 tiling normal map (sizes do not fit). Not tested: textures above 1024 pixels, non-multiple-of-64x32 2D mips, 8888 byte order, sRGB. Nothing in the engine yet; nothing committed.

## 2026-10-29 (104): FM6 IDx step 3: `.modelbin` mesh decoder in `fh1-formats`
New `crates/fh1-formats/src/modelbin.rs` (layout in its doc comment) and probe `examples/modelbin_probe.rs` (checks every modelbin under a folder; optionally writes one OBJ of all LOD0 meshes).
**Format found (from the bytes, checked on all IDx files):** buffers `IndB` / `VerB` with a 16-byte header (count, bytes, stride, components, DXGI format); a shared position stream (`VerB` id -1, four signed 16-bit values per vertex) plus one attribute stream per vertex range (the stream sizes add up exactly to the position count, 17,228 on the body); `VLay` vertex layouts (POSITION, NORMAL, TEXCOORD 0-3, TANGENT 0-1 with DXGI formats); `Mesh` records with material slot, LOD mask / LOD number, first index, base vertex, index count, a stream binding list and, at the end, five texture-coordinate rects, the position scale and the position offset. Material slot 0xFFFF marks merged shadow-only copies. Six LODs per part.
**Checks:** 81 of 82 files decode (the skeleton file has no meshes and returns none); 317 LOD0 meshes, 177,184 triangles. Decoded box against the box stored in each mesh: largest difference 0.0000 m. Normal = normalize(position.w, normal.x, normal.y): mean cosine with the triangle normals 0.984 over 177,171 triangles (octahedral decoding, tried first, gave 0.0). The body parts are already in car space; the assembled exterior is 4.08 m long and 1.76 m wide.
**Not done / not verified:** `ProxyLOD.modelbin` (a 48-byte vertex layout the parser does not handle); which of the five rects belongs to which texture-coordinate set (UVs are returned raw); tangents; morph buffers (`MBuf`, probably damage); skinning; doors, wipers, wheels, brakes and control arms are stored around their own pivot and need the skeleton (`Skel` chunk / skeleton file, not decoded) or `Locators.xml` to be placed; materials (`MatI` chunks name `.materialbin` files in the game's shared `_library`, not read). Nothing in the engine; nothing committed.

## 2026-10-30 (107): FM6 IDx: wheel morphs decoded (rim sized like the game does); tyre geometry not found (stopped after two searches)
`modelbin.rs` now reads morphs: `Mrph` (target names) and `MBuf` (per vertex, half floats: position change per target, then normal change per target); `Mesh::morphs`. `fm6_car_probe` sizes the wheel with these morphs instead of scaling it, solving the two weights from the model itself (no constants in the code).
**What the morphs do (checked):** the wheel model has two targets, `weight[0]` and `weight[1]`. `weight[1]` moves every vertex out from the axle by 1.3182 x its distance (spread +-0.0003 over four meshes): a 0.14 m (11-inch) rim at weight 0, 0.324 m (25.5-inch) at weight 1. `weight[0]` moves only the barrel's inner edge (x = 1.0) along the axle by -0.9 (fit error 0.0000): barrel width 1.0 - 0.9 x weight. For the IDx's 19-inch rim and 225 mm width the weights are 0.552 and 0.861; the placed rims then span exactly 0.241 m around each wheel centre and the spoke face keeps its depth (the earlier scaling squashed it). The rim width = tyre width is INFERRED (the database has no rim width). The rule "a morph buffer with one target holds the last target" is inferred from the wheel's two blur meshes only.
**Tyre (not found, two searches):** (1) all of `media` (every zip under 400 MB, including all 212 car-library zips, and file names) holds no tyre model; (2) the executable's strings have no tyre model path; its tyre strings are physics settings (`TireRadius`, sidewall height and thickness, tread mass) and one query, `SELECT %s AS TireModelName FROM %s WHERE Id = %d`. The database table `List_UpgradeTireCompound` gives the IDx stock tyre "model" `b` (wet `a`); the car library has matching materials `genericAssetMaterials/tireTread_b` and `tireSidewall_b`. Most likely the game builds the tyre shape in code from the sizes and textures it with these materials; that code was not looked for. Nothing in the engine; nothing committed.
